Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
4Q NB build forecast cut 5% despite October’s beat: October ODM NB shipments
came in 4% above MSe, but we lower our 4Q26 build forecast by 5% to 21.9M units
(-13% q/q, -32% y/y) to reflect the latest ODM guidance. The revised forecast implies

limited quarter-end pull-in strength and a deeper y/y contraction than the 26%
decline in 3Q26. Despite an already depressed 3Q base, the projected 13%
sequential decline is well below the 15-year average of flat q/q shipments in 4Q.
PC outlook seen continuing to weaken into 2027: NB build trends have weakened
over recent months, while increasingly cautious ODM commentary provides further
evidence of pressure on PC demand. Asustek expects global PC unit shipments to

decline 10–15% y/y in 2027 (link), while Quanta expects global NB shipments to
drop by high single digits to 10% y/y (link). We believe 2027 outlooks from the
supply chain have worsened in recent months, and both outlooks are directionally
consistent with our forecast for an 11.5% y/y decline in global PC units in 2027.
Looking to 1Q27, our preliminary view is a further sequential decline in NB builds,
although we have yet to establish a formal forecast.

Key Takeaways
Top five ODMs' October 2026 NB shipments were 7.1M units (-27% m/m, -30% y/
y), 4% above our estimate.
We see November 2026 NB builds at 7.3M units (+4% m/m, -30% y/y).
We lower our 4Q26 NB build estimate by 5% to 21.9M units (-13% q/q, -32% y/y).
Our top five ODMs' CY26 NB build estimate is ~106.6M units (-17% y/y).
November 15, 2026 09:00 PM GMT

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Elite Material
Valuation
Our target price for EMC is set at NT$7,400, based on a target PE multiple of 28x on our 2027E EPS average. We believe our
target PE multiple, at the peak of its forward PE average in the past 5 years, is justified by a strong earnings outlook supported
by its leading position in CCL supply for AI GPU and ASIC (high m/s) from 2026E onwards. We also believe the continued spec

upgrade for CCL, price hikes and product mix enhancement should help support its margin expansion going forward. At our
target price, the shares would trade at 55x/28x 2026E/27E PB.
Risks
Citi’s quant system rates EMC high-risk given high share price volatility, which we largely attribute to frequent debates over the
spec upgrade for next-gen AI servers. In general, we believe some investors initially tend to have high hope for aggressive spec
upgrade without sufficiently considering the scalability and cost structure of CCL products, which then leads to a shortfall in

the end. However, from a longer-term perspective, we see a decent and clear spec upgrade trend with meaningful ASP growth
potential among AI servers of different generations. As such, we do not assign a high-risk rating.
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target price include: 1) a slower-than-expected CCL
upgrade trend; 2) weaker-than-expected AI server demand; 3) production bottlenecks in AI supply chain (eg, glass, copper foil,

foundry or OSAT); 4) unexpected share loss in key AI server projects; and 5) stricter practice of OOC policy by end customers.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리 사이클SK하이닉스(000660) 높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리_0a1416f9.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29

### Page 1

2026년 10월 29일 
기업분석

SK하이닉스 (000660) 
높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리 사이클

Buy(유지) 
목표주가 3,150,000원, 현재 주가(10/28): 1,487,000원 
▶ Analyst 박준영 jyp94@hanwha.com 3772-7481 / RA 김나우 now.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7710

3Q26 Review: HBM4 출하 확대와 예상보다 강한 가격 상승 
분기 매출액 104.6조원(QoQ +32%, YoY +328%), 영업이익 83.3조원(QoQ +38%, YoY +632%)을 기록하며 시장 기대
치를 상회함. 영업이익률은 전분기 대비 약 3%p 상승한 80%로 사상 최대 분기 실적을 달성함. 특히 DRAM ASP가 전
분기 대비 20% 후반 상승하며 당사 기존 예상치인 +23%를 상회한 점에 주목할 필요가 있음. NAND ASP 역시 20% 
중반 상승하며 예상보다 강한 가격 흐름을 나타냄. DRAM ASP 상승에는 기존 DRAM의 가격 인상과 함께 3분기부터 
본격화된 HBM4 양산 및 출하 확대에 따른 제품 믹스 개선 효과가 반영됨. 서버 DDR5 판매량 역시 전분기 대비 40% 
증가하며 실적 성장을 뒷받침함. 특히 HBM4는 기존 HBM3E 대비 높은 기술적 난도를 반영한 가격으로 공급되고 있어 
향후 출하 비중 확대에 따른 추가적인 ASP 상승 효과도 기대됨. 
이번 실적에서 중요한 부분은 예상보다 높은 가격 상승폭이 2027년 실적 추정치의 상향 가능성을 높이고 있다는 점임. 3
분기 DRAM ASP가 기존 추정치를 상회한 가운데 4분기에도 HBM4 출하 확대와 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 이어질 
것으로 예상되기 때문임. 동사는 4분기 DRAM 출하량이 전분기 대비 20% 중반 증가할 것으로 전망했으며, HBM4 출
하량이 HBM3E를 넘어 주력 제품으로 자리 잡을 것으로 예상함. 특히 HBM4의 양산 수율이 당초 목표를 상회하고 있
으며 높은 고객 수요에 대응하기 위해 생산성 극대화에 집중하고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. HBM4 출하 확대에 따른 믹스 
개선과 기존 DRAM 가격 상승이 동시에 진행되는 가운데 HBM과 일반 DRAM의 수익성 격차 역시 유의미하게 축소
될 것으로 예상됨. 이는 특정 제품에 대한 수익성 의존도가 낮아지면서 DRAM 사업 전반의 이익 체력이 강화되고 있음
을 의미함. 메모리 산업의 높은 영업 레버리지를 고려하면 예상보다 높은 ASP와 출하량 증가는 2027년 이익 추정치의 
추가 상향으로 연결될 가능성이 높다고 판단함.

공급 부족의 장기화와 LTA 확대, 메모리 산업의 이익 구조 변화 
이번 컨퍼런스콜에서 확인된 또 다른 중요한 변화는 공급 부족의 장기화와 장기공급계약(LTA) 확대임. 동사는 첨단 공정
의 기술적 난도 상승과 신규 생산시설 구축에 소요되는 시간을 고려할 때 메모리 공급이 수요를 충족하지 못하는 환경이 
2030년까지 지속될 것으로 전망함. 특히 글로벌 주요 고객사 14곳과 장기공급계약을 체결했으며, 2027~2028년 전체 생
산능력의 50% 이상에 해당하는 물량을 확보함. HBM의 경우 2029년까지의 물량이 이미 판매 확정된 상황으로 중장기 
수요 가시성이 상당히 높아졌다고 판단함. 주목할 부분은 LTA가 단순한 공급물량 확보를 넘어 메모리 업체의 사업 구조
와 이익 안정성을 변화시키고 있다는 점임. 계약 가격은 고객 및 제품별로 차이가 있으나 합의된 범위 내에서 시장 상황
을 반영하는 구조로 운영되고 있으며 예치금 등 실질적인 계약 이행력을 높이는 장치도 포함됨. 이는 장기적인 판매물량
을 확보하면서도 업황 상승에 따른 가격 인상 효과를 일정 부분 누릴 수 있다는 점에서 긍정적임.

### Page 2

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

2 
특히 전체 생산능력의 50% 이상을 LTA로 확보하면서 나머지 물량은 시장 상황에 따라 탄력적으로 대응할 수 있다는 점
에 주목할 필요가 있음. 장기계약을 통해 안정적인 매출 기반을 확보하는 동시에 미계약 물량을 활용해 추가적인 가격 
상승에 대응할 수 있기 때문임. 물론 장기계약이 메모리 가격 하락을 완전히 방어하는 것은 아니지만, 과거와 비교해 물
량 가시성과 고객사와의 협상력이 동시에 개선되고 있다는 점은 분명함. 과거 메모리 산업이 높은 이익을 창출하는 국면
에서도 낮은 밸류에이션을 적용 받았던 이유 중 하나는 이익의 지속성에 대한 불확실성이었음. 그러나 장기계약 확대와 
공급 제약의 장기화가 동시에 진행된다면 시장이 평가하는 이익의 지속성 역시 높아질 수 있음. 따라서 향후 주가 상승
은 단순한 EPS 추정치 상향뿐 아니라 이익 변동성 축소에 따른 밸류에이션 재평가가 동시에 진행되는 형태로 나타날 가
능성이 높다고 판단함. 
AI 인프라 투자에 대한 우려 역시 중장기 메모리 수요를 훼손할 가능성은 제한적이라고 판단함. 동사는 주요 하이퍼스케
일러의 클라우드 매출이 전년 대비 30~80% 성장하고 있으며 기업용 AI 솔루션 계약 확대와 클라우드 수주잔고 증가를 
통해 AI 투자의 사업적 성과가 확인되고 있다고 설명함. 특히 초기 AI 투자가 소수 빅테크에 집중되었던 것과 달리 최근
에는 일반기업의 자체 인프라 구축, 소버린 AI, 온프레미스 등으로 투자 주체가 다변화되고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. 특정 
사업자의 투자 속도 조절이 전체 AI 인프라 수요에 미치는 영향은 과거 대비 완화될 수 있기 때문임. AI 투자에 대한 우
려가 지속되는 상황에서도 실제 메모리 수요와 고객사들의 장기계약 확대가 확인되고 있다는 점에서 메모리 업황의 구조
적 성장에 대한 긍정적인 시각을 유지함.

HBM4 경쟁력 강화와 CAPEX 확대, 이익 성장과 주주환원의 선순환 
HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대와 신규 생산능력 확보 역시 2027년 실적 성장의 중요한 기반이 될 전망임. 동사는 M15X
의 생산능력을 연말까지 최대 수준으로 확대할 계획이며 HBM4의 양산 수율 역시 당초 목표를 상회하는 등 안정적인 
생산체계를 확보하고 있음. 특히 HBM 수요가 공급능력의 두 배를 상회하는 상황에서 고객사들의 높은 선호가 이어지고 
있다는 점은 경쟁력 유지에 긍정적임. 최근 일부 고객사들이 HBM 적층 단수를 낮추는 방안을 검토하고 있으나 이는 공
급 부족 상황에서 제한된 물량을 효율적으로 활용하기 위한 전략으로 판단됨. 적층 단수 조정이 전체 HBM 수요 감소로 
직결되는 것은 아니며 오히려 후공정 생산능력에 대한 요구가 확대될 가능성도 존재함. HBM4E와 커스텀 HBM 개발 
역시 고객사 일정에 맞춰 진행되고 있어 차세대 제품에서도 경쟁력을 유지할 것으로 기대됨. 
한편 2027년 CAPEX는 선단 공정 전환과 용인 클러스터를 비롯한 국내외 생산 인프라 확충으로 전년 대비 증가할 전망
임. 다만 CAPEX 확대를 과거와 같은 공급 과잉의 신호로 해석할 필요는 없다고 판단함. 선단 공정의 기술적 난도 상승
과 고부가 제품 비중 확대에 따라 투자금액 증가가 실제 생산량 증가로 정비례하지 않기 때문임. 특히 동사는 신규 팹 
건설 일정을 앞당기면서도 클린룸은 순차적으로 개방하고 실제 생산능력 확대는 최종 수요와 수익성을 고려해 단계적으
로 진행할 계획임. 고객사의 투자 계획뿐 아니라 재무건전성과 자금조달 여력, 공급망 준비 상황까지 점검해 CAPEX 집
행에 반영한다는 점도 긍정적임. 이는 AI 인프라 투자 확대에 대응하면서도 과거 메모리 산업의 공급 과잉 위험을 최소
화하기 위한 전략으로 판단함. 
동사는 이에 더해 2026년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원 규모의 주주환원 계획을 발표했음. 현금배당 30조원 이상, 자사주 
매입 및 소각 60조원 이상으로 구성되며, 기존에 약속했던 잉여현금흐름(FCF)의 50% 이상을 주주에게 환원한다는 원칙
을 구체화한 것임. 지난 8월 발표한 40조원 규모의 자사주 매입은 10월 21일 완료됐으며, 해당 주식은 향후 소각될 예정
임. 이번 3분기 이사회에서는 현금배당 14조원과 추가 자사주 매입 20조원을 결의했으며, 자사주 매입액 중 10조원은 전
량 소각하고 나머지 10조원은 임직원 보상에 활용할 계획임. 임직원 보상용 자사주는 주주환원 규모에서 제외됨. 특히 
향후에도 자사주 매입을 지속할 계획이라는 점에서 주주환원 정책의 지속성이 강화됐다는 판단임.

### Page 3

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

3 
3분기 주당 배당금은 2만원으로, 실적 개선을 반영한 일반배당 1만원과 키옥시아 투자수익을 활용한 특별배당 1만원으
로 구성됨. 총 배당 규모는 약 14조원이며 회사는 올해 실현한 키옥시아 투자수익의 대부분을 3분기 및 연말 특별배당을 
통해 주주에게 환원할 계획임. 이번 결정은 메모리 가격 상승과 고부가 제품 확대에 따른 현금창출력 개선이 실질적인 
주주환원 확대로 연결되고 있음을 보여줌. 특히 2027년 설비투자 확대가 예상되는 상황에서도 대규모 현금배당과 자사
주 소각을 병행한다는 점은 재무적 여력에 대한 자신감을 반영하는 것으로 판단됨. 향후에도 안정적인 실적 성장과 FCF 
확대가 이어질 경우 주주환원의 지속 가능성이 높아질 전망이며 이는 과거 대비 높아진 이익 창출력과 함께 중장기 밸류
에이션 재평가를 뒷받침하는 요인으로 작용할 것으로 예상됨. 
결국 이번 실적발표에서 확인된 핵심은 예상보다 강한 메모리 가격 상승과 HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대, 그리고 장기계
약을 통한 이익 가시성 개선임. 특히 HBM4의 높은 가격과 안정적인 양산 수율, 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 동시에 확
인되면서 2027년 이익 추정치의 추가 상향 가능성이 높아졌다고 판단함. 여기에 LTA 확대에 따른 중장기 이익 안정성 
개선과 대규모 주주환원까지 더해지고 있음. 실적 추정치 상향과 이익의 지속성 개선이 동시에 진행되고 있다는 점에서 
긍정적인 투자 시각을 유지함.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10 월 29 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성됐음을 확인합니다. 
본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 자료를 
기관투자자 또는 제3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (박준영, 김나우) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 5

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

5 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

### Page 1

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test  
78 
삼성전자  (005930) 
2026.11.04

STRESS TEST 
[반도체/전기전자] 고의영 
2122-9179 ey.ko@imfnsec.com

목표주가 37만원, ‘28년 기준으로는 56만원까지 열림 
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 37만원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. ‘27년 BPS에 목표 
P/B 2.3배를 적용했다. 이는 ‘00년 이후 25년간 평균 P/B의 +2표준편차에 
해당한다[표1]. ‘26~’28년 ROE는 각각 56%, 59%, 48%로 지난 25년 평균인 
22%를 크게 상회할 전망이며, 고원형 ROE가 장기간 유지될 것으로 판단한다.  
이익 지속성에 대한 믿음이 공고해질 경우 시장의 시야는 ‘28년으로 향할 것이다. 
이 경우 ‘28년 BPS 기준 목표주가는 56만원까지 열릴 수 있다[표2]. 
동사 주가는 ‘26년 6월 고점 대비 10월 저점 기준 43% 하락해 과거 다운사이클 
평균 하락폭인 -41%를 넘어섰다. 특히 과거 8~13개월에 걸쳐 진행됐던 조정이 
이번에는 4개월 만에 나타났다. 시장이 ‘28년 이익 급감을 상정하고 있는 것이다. 
이러한 우려가 지속되는 한, 이익의 눈높이가 높아질수록 멀티플은 저렴해질 
뿐이며, DRAM 가격이 예상보다 더 올라갈 것이라는 소식이 주가의 촉매로 
작용하지 못한다. 결국 주가 반등을 위해서는 시장이 상정하고 있는 다운사이클이 
우려만큼 깊지 않을 것이라는 믿음이 필요한 것으로 본다.

STRESS TEST와 HBM 재평가 
① 당사는 고객들이 어느 정도 가격대에서 LTA를 해지하려는 유인이 생길지 
추정해봤다. Micron의 코멘트를 차용하여 ‘22~’23년 수준의 스트레스 테스트를 
시나리오 분석한 결과, 4Q27 고점 대비 DRAM 가격이 60% 수준 빠져야 해지가 
검토될 수 있다[그림2]. 그마저도 다음 공급 부족기의 물량 배정 우선순위 상실 
등 정성적 요인까지 감안하면 실제 해지 가능성은 더욱 낮은 것으로 판단한다. 
② HBM 비중이 높아질수록 동사의 이익 변동성은 낮아질 것이다. 특히 
중장기적으로 확대될 cHBM은 고객별 수주생산 제품으로 현물시장과 유통 
재고가 없고, 생산에 앞서 물량과 가격이 결정된다. 수요가 둔화되더라도 범용 
메모리처럼 재고 누적과 가격 급락으로 이어지기보다는 발주 조정이나 계약 
재협상을 통해 영향을 받는 구조다.  
동사 HBM 매출은 ‘26년 28조원에서 ‘28년 215조원으로 증가할 전망이다. 
‘27년 HBM 가격 방향성의 윤곽이 잡힌 현 시점에서, 동사 HBM 매출의 변수는 
가격이 아니라 물량이며, 물량은 고객과 사전에 협의되는 구조다. 과거 메모리 
업황에서는 2년 뒤의 이익을 가늠하기 어려웠으나, 이제는 HBM을 통해 중장기 
실적을 논의할 수 있게 됐다는 점, 그 자체에 주목한다. 
만약 동사의 ‘26~’28년 HBM 영업이익에 시기별 TSMC P/E (21배, 15배, 12배)를 
적용하면 HBM 가치는 동사 시가총액의 19%, 69%, 108%를 설명한다[표3]. 
특히 cHBM이 본격화할 ‘28년에는 HBM만으로 현재 시가총액이 설명될 수 
있다는 의미다. 시장이 통상 받아들이는 10배를 타겟팅해도 결과는 크게 다르지 
않다.

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test  
79

‘27년 주가 경로: 회복, 박스권, 재평가 
연말까지는 급락 이후 저점을 높여가는 회복 구간일 전망이다.  
‘27년 상반기까지는 ‘28년 업황 지속성에 대한 우려가 상존할 전망이다. 이 불확실성이 
멀티플을 누르는 동안 ‘27년 이익은 계속 상향되므로 EPS 상승과 멀티플 하락이 상쇄되는 
구간이 이어질 전망이다. 기회비용은 결국 환원수익률로 보상받아야 하므로, ‘27년 1월 신규 
주주환원 정책이 중요한 변곡점이 될 것으로 판단한다.  
당사는 동사가 ‘27~’29년 FCF의 50%를 주주에게 환원하고, 이 중 70%를 배당, 30%를 
자사주 매입/소각으로 쓰는 것으로 가정하여, 현재 주가 기준 ‘27년 15% 수준의 
주주환원수익률을 기대한다. 미국 10년물 국채 금리가 5% 안팎인 환경에서 무위험 금리의 
3배에 달하는 환원수익률, 그중 배당만으로 10% 수준의 수익률은 그 자체로 희소한 투자 
포인트로 볼 수 있다. 
‘27년 하반기 이후에는, 실적 추정의 기준 연도가 ‘28년으로 넘어가고, 이후 신규 팹 물량이 
소량 출하되면서 센티먼트상 우려는 커질 것이다. 그러나 LTA가 이익을 지킨다는 사실이 
숫자로 확인되고 cHBM의 가치가 재평가된다면 순환 할인이 축소될 것으로 판단한다.

업사이드 요인 
① AI 투자 지속성 우려로 최근 주가 하락이 가팔랐던 만큼, 이 부분에 대한 우려가 
완화되면 반등의 속도와 폭이 예상보다 빠를 수 있다 
② 향후 주주환원 규모가 예상보다 더 적극적일 경우 추가적인 업사이드가 될 수 있다. 
혹은 주주환원 이후에도 남는 대규모 FCF의 활용 방안도 촉매가 될 수 있다.

 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견]

 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상

Document 294: AI避风港！一片惨跌声中，印度股市11月涨逾12%领跑全球

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI避风港！一片惨跌声中，印度股市11月涨逾12%领跑全球.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-26
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

11 ⽉ 25 ⽇，印度 Sensex 指数收报 83938 点， Nifty 50 收报 25819.25 点，延续强势。 11 ⽉以
来，印度股市累计涨幅超过 12% ，在全球 90 多个主要市场中排名第⼀。低 AI 敞⼝令印度成
为本轮全球科技股抛售潮的避⻛港，外资单⽉净流⼊ 142.8 亿美元有望创纪录。
摘要：
当全球 AI 科技股遭遇抛售、亚太市场普遍下挫之际，印度股市却凭借低 AI 敞⼝的 “ 意外优势 ” ，成
为本轮⾏情中最⼤的受益者。
11 ⽉ 25 ⽇，印度 Sensex 指数收报 83938 点， Nifty 50 收报 25819.25 点，单⽇涨幅均超过 1.8% 。
11 ⽉以来，两⼤基准指数累计涨幅已超过 12% ，在全球 90 多个主要市场中排名第⼀。与此同
时，外国投资者本⽉已向印度股市净流⼊ 142.8 亿美元，有望创下单⽉流⼊纪录。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

同⽇，亚太科技股集体重挫。韩国 KOSPI 指数单⽇收跌 6.16% ，三星电⼦跌 9.87% ， SK 海⼒⼠
跌 11.05% ；⽇经 225 指数收跌 2.78% ，软银集团跌近 15% 。印度与周边市场的⾛势分化，在这⼀
天被放⼤⾄极致。
低 AI 敞⼝，从 “ 劣势 ” 变 “ 优势 ”
随着市场开始质疑⾼企的 AI 估值和巨额资本开⽀计划能否持续，全球资⾦加速从 AI 相关股票中撤
出，转向与 AI 产业关联度较低的市场。印度因此成为这⼀轮资⾦轮动的主要⽬的地。
据路透社数据， 11 ⽉外国投资者已向印度股市净流⼊ 142.8 亿美元，⼤量资⾦集中流向印度市值
最⼤的蓝筹公司，包括⼤型银⾏和综合企业集团。
分析师对此直⾔： “ 过去两年，印度市场表现落后，原因很简单，就是缺乏 AI 产业敞⼝。但在当
前这个时点，这反⽽成为了⼀项优势。 ”
美国银⾏上周发布的亚洲基⾦经理调查报告也印证了这⼀趋势 —— 受访基⾦经理普遍⼤幅削减
了对东亚半导体板块及全球泛科技股的超配⽐例，⽽印度股票市场则⾃今年 1 ⽉以来⾸次转为净

超配，净超配⽐例升⾄ 5% 。
企业盈利超预期，基本⾯提供⽀撑
资⾦⾯之外，企业盈利的改善为这轮反弹提供了另⼀重⽀撑。
野村控股印度分部在⼀份报告中指出，其追踪的 264 家印度企业，截⾄ 9 ⽉的第三季度净利润同
⽐增⻓ 31% ，较市场⼀致预期⾼出 14 个百分点。即便剔除⽯油天然⽓企业和⾦属⽣产商，盈利
仍同⽐增⻓ 23% ，超出市场预期 5 个百分点。
与此同时，国际油价的回落也为印度市场提供了额外利好。由于印度⼤部分原油需求依赖进
⼝，油价下跌有助于缓解进⼝成本、通胀及经常账⼾⾚字压⼒，并对企业利润率和卢⽐汇率形
成正⾯⽀撑。

Document 301: Anthropic是下一个微软？这家投行分析师吵翻了！

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic是下一个微软？这家投行分析师吵翻了！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-26
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Hedgeye 分析师 Freedman 看多⾄ 92 美元，押注 Claude 成为⽩领 “ 微软式操作系统 ” ，靠效
率提升与算⼒成本回落双重降本撑起利润率； Wang 做空⾄ 59 美元，警告数据中⼼融资趋
紧、算⼒瓶颈卡住变现， token 降价需靠⽤量补，并下调明年营收预期⾄ 1200 亿。
摘要：
同⼀家华尔街研究机构内部，两位股票分析师对当前市场最热⻔的新股 Anthropic PBC 给出了
截然相反的判断。Hedgeye Risk Management 的通信与软件分析师 Andrew Freedman 认为，该
股当前价位对⻓期投资者具有吸引⼒，公允价值⽬标为 92 美元；他的同事、全球科技分析师
Felix Wang 则在周⼆的报告中建议做空，⽬标价 59 美元。两⼈的分歧，折射出市场对 AI 颠覆性
潜⼒及其核⼼公司估值⽅式的深层裂痕。
据彭博 11 ⽉ 26 ⽇报道，Wang 的悲观逻辑集中在算⼒融资与需求两端。他认为， Anthropic 所依
赖的数据中⼼正⾯临⽇益困难的融资环境，投资者对 AI 基础设施项⽬的态度趋于谨慎，这⼀点已

从 CoreWeave 等新型云服务商融资成本飙升中得到印证。算⼒容量受限将制约 Anthropic 的产
品变现能⼒。同时，来⾃ OpenAI 等对⼿的激烈竞争可能压低 AI token 价格，需要靠使⽤量增⻓
来弥补。他预计 Anthropic 明年营收为 1200 亿美元，低于分析师普遍预期的 1360 亿美元。
Freedman 的乐观则押注于平台化前景与短期需求信号。他表⽰， Anthropic 有 15% ⾄ 20% 的概
率成为⽩领⼯作者的 “ 微软式操作系统 ”—— 不仅出售 Claude 模型输出的 “ 原始智能 ” ，还提供员⼯
⽇常使⽤的插件和应⽤。他还援引实时数据称，尽管公司警告冬季假期期间使⽤量可能下滑，
但 Anthropic 的营收⾃上次披露数据以来持续增⻓。
这场内部分歧已公开化。Wang 发布看空观点后， Freedman 在社交媒体上与其划清界限，称⾃
⼰ “ 未参与导致该决定的研究或流程 ” 。
Anthropic 的股价⾛势本⾝即体现了这种分歧。该股上市后估值⼀度飙升⾄ 2 万亿美元，随后⼤幅

回落。周三盘中⼀度跌破 74 美元，此后回升⾄ 83 美元的 IPO 发⾏价附近。上市⾸⽇股价曾触及
115 美元。

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
多空双⽅的完整逻辑链
看多⾯：平台化与双重降本。Freedman 认为，⻓期看 Anthropic 同时受益于两项成本节约：
他相信，即便公司下调 AI 使⽤定价，这仍将⽀撑其利润率。 “ 关键在于谁拥有⼯作的操作系统， ”
他说， “ 这就是 Anthropic 的看多逻辑 —— 它成为⼀个平台，⻓期看变得类似微软。 ”
看空⾯：算⼒瓶颈与需求存疑。Wang 承认前沿模型可能催⽣药物发现等新突破，但表⽰ “ 很难给
出时间表 ” ，这类收⼊ “ 尚未体现在今天的数字⾥ ” 。
他同时担忧，算⼒不⾜将直接限制变现空间，⽽ token 价格下⾏需要靠使⽤量增⻓来抵消，他对
需求是否⾜够存疑。
华尔街机构内部对同⼀问题得出不同结论此前亦有先例，例如 2023 年摩根⼠丹利策略师与投资
组合经理对⼤盘前景给出的对⽴观点。但⼀般⽽⾔，华尔街机构的研究团队会形成共识观点，

尤其是在个股层⾯。
在独⽴研究机构 Hedgeye ， Freedman 是覆盖 Anthropic 的⾸席分析师，但这家 AI 公司庞杂的业务
也延伸⾄ Wang 所覆盖的⾏业领域。
Freedman 的观点更贴近华尔街对 Anthropic 的整体热情。彭博追踪的 17 位分析师中，有 15 位给
予该股买⼊评级。 Freedman 实际上是仅有的两位给出持有评级的分析师之⼀。
他在采访中表⽰，持有三到六个⽉期限的短期投资者或许应等待更便宜的⼊场点，原因是
Anthropic 的部分投资者持股在 IPO 后设有数⽉锁定期，随着锁定期到期，未来⼏个⽉可能形成
抛售压⼒。
⼀是随着计算效率提升，训练新模型和回答查询的成本逐年下降；⼆是算⼒成本在 “ 需求收紧⻅
顶 ” 后将回落。

Document 360: First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impression: RBNZ survey of inflation expectations,
December quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
13:50 November 17 2026 Share
Inflation expectations remained contained in the RBNZ’s latest survey. High oil prices and
elevated near-term inflation don’t appear to have boosted medium-term inflation
expectations.
RBNZ inflation expectations survey

### Page 2

1 year ahead: 2.92% (Prev: 2.60%, up 32bps)
2 years ahead: 2.36% (Prev: 2.34%, up 2bps)
5 years ahead: 2.17% (Prev: 2.31%, down 14bps)
10 years ahead: 2.13% (Prev: 2.20%, down 7bp)

There was only limited movement in the RBNZ’s latest survey of inflation expectations. 
Importantly for the RBNZ’s policy deliberations ahead of their December policy meeting, the detail of
today’s update points to inflation expectations remaining well contained despite the current oil-related
lift in inflation.

Looking at the details of the December inflation expectations update:
Expectations for inflation 1 year ahead did rise to 2.92% (vs 2.60% last quarter). That’s consistent with
the sharp rise in fuel prices in recent months, which we expect will push overall inflation up to 4.3% by
the end of this year. 
However, expectations for inflation at longer horizons were much more stable. Notably, the closely
watched measure of inflation in two years’ time nudged just slightly higher, rising to 2.36% (vs 2.34%
previously). 
Similarly, expectations for inflation at long horizons (5 and 10 years ahead) actually slipped back this
quarter and now sit just a little above 2%.

Implications for the RBNZ
Today’s result will be welcome news for the RBNZ. With oil prices pushing inflation to high levels, there
was a risk that expectations for inflation would take a step higher, adding to the pressure on prices over
the year ahead. Such risks had been alluded to in a recent speech by the RBNZ’s Chief Economist.
Nevertheless, the RBNZ is still looking at an elevated near-term inflation outlook. Importantly, the
strength in inflation isn’t just due to high oil prices. Core inflation has remained at firm levels for an
extended period. 
Consistent with those firm underlying inflation pressures, we continue to expect an OCR hike at the
RBNZ’s upcoming 9 December policy meeting. 
We’ll discuss our thoughts in more detail in our latest Economic Overview, out tomorrow.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

► 伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝組織持續攻擊沙烏
地阿 拉 伯 和荷 姆 茲 海峽 船 隻 ，美 期 中 選舉
前，地緣政治風險不減，添股市賣壓。中國
長鑫存儲在新型晶片研發取得進展，衝擊半
導體類股，美光與三星、海力士記憶體廠即
使仍供不應求，亦遭市場拋售，南韓股指週
跌幅逾18% 。 網 通 晶 片 大 廠 博 通 執 行 長 表

示：資金是AI建設擴張面臨的最大瓶頸，光
通訊股領跌走弱，科技、半導體股重挫。
► 美股第三季財報登場，花旗、高盛本季淨利
優於預期，但富國銀行淨利息收入失色，摩
根大通自2023年4Q以來，首次獲利不如預
期，美銀亦不佳。艾斯摩爾上修全年營收展
望，透露部分客戶已洽詢2029年至2030年設
備訂單，顯示晶片擴產需求仍強。台積電3Q
毛利率達66.4%較前月略減，因2奈米快速放
量與海外廠產能利用率造成稀釋，但今年資
本支出財測維持600到640億美元不變，反映
代理AI客戶需求強勁，預期2026年至2028年
資本支出將較過去三年進一步顯著提高。

► 美國9 月CPI 、PPI 皆上升， 且 超 過 市 場 預
期，9月PPI月增率為1.3%，創2022年來最
大漲幅，高於市場預期的月增0.5%，年增率
達6.3%，為2022年12月來最大漲幅，顯示
通膨壓力正從能源擴大至其他領域。通膨僵
固性使市場預期高利率將維持更久時間，美
國公債殖利率一度走高，但隨股市重挫、避
險資金轉入，公債止跌回升。體質較弱的企
業債出現警訊，CoreWeave債券殖利率大幅
攀升， 2032 年 到 期 債 券 殖 利 率 一 度 升 至
15%，反應公司舉債擴建資料中心，市場擔
憂其債 務規 模、 利 息負擔 及未來 再融 資能
力，除違約風險上升，過高成本侵蝕企業獲
利、股債皆挫，顯示目前即使是AI、科技信
用債表現也開始出現分化。
► 中東地緣風險推升油價上漲，儘管川普宣布

州油價仍處於每桶98美元高位，推升全球通
膨預期。虛擬資產比特幣、以太幣隨風險性
資產同步下挫。

► 美國9月CPI年增率上升至3.7%，核心CPI年
增率由2.4%升至2.6%，核心月增率0.3%，
高於預期值0.2%。能源是通膨反彈的主因，
貢獻整體CPI月增幅超過一半；能源衝擊雖
為主導，但消費韌性使企業保有成本轉嫁能
力，住房與部分服務價格維持黏性，需求面
通膨略有提高，加上企業資本支出提升經濟
與企業獲利韌性，使總需求不易快速降溫。
► 凱投預期Fed 12月將升息1碼，明年3月再升
1碼，政策利率可能在4%以上維持更久。受
到油價高盤、通膨僵固，與財政惡化、政府
發債籌資逢企業債搶錢等影響，美歐日主要
國家公 債利 率近 期 來到高 位 。其中， 法國
2027年財政預算欲刪減支出(包括養老金、公
部門工資等)引發民眾反彈，衝擊主權信用評
等，法國CDS與法德10年期公債利差擴大、
歐元走弱，美元轉強，商品、黃金承壓。法
國政治不確定性讓歐股修正、法股最疲，成
熟國家公債殖利率升高，增加企業借貸成本
與股市評價壓力，續為金融市場擾動因子。

資料來源：Bloomberg，Factset，Goldman Sachs，凱基證券整理，註：右上圖之大型雲端服務供應商(CSP)企業為Amazon、Alphabet、Microsoft、Oracle
► 美國第三季財報季展開，市場將觀察企業在
高利率環境下的獲利表現。根據FactSet數據
顯示，標普500指數第三季獲利成長預估達
29.5%，顯示企業基本面仍具支撐，其中科
技 產業 受 惠AI需求帶動， 獲 利 成 長預 估達
65%，持續扮演企業獲利的重要成長動能。
► 聚焦科技類股，AI資本支出仍為本輪企業獲
利成長的主要驅動力。高盛指出，第三季AI

基礎建設相關企業將貢獻標普500獲利增長
的54%。此外，大型CSP之雲端業務營收有
望加速成長，反映AI運算及雲端服務需求持
續提升，有助支撐後續資本支出擴張動能。
► 近期市場波動仍受高利率環境影響，壓抑投
資人風險偏好與估值擴張空間，顯示市場目
前壓力更多來自評價面而非基本面。隨財報
數據陸續公布，若企業持續繳出優於預期的
獲利表現，並維持正向財測，市場有望逐步
回歸基本面評價，使企業獲利成長逐步支撐
股市表現。
美企財報獲利穩健，對股市中長期有撐

美股第三季獲利成長持續上修，科技為主要支撐 CSP雲端營收加速，資本支出變現跡象持續受驗證
評價面疑慮下，股價難以進一步擴張
熱門議題財報展望

投資焦點
► 醫療保健、醫療服務與生物技術等行業，在
景氣不好時，市場預期民眾對醫療保健產業
的需求 不易 下降 ， 成為傳 統防禦 型類 股代
表。目前金融環境利率偏高，11月美國總統
大選國會恐變天，股市波動風險上升，觀察
過去當VIX恐慌指數上揚時，醫療保健產業
相較於科技股更穩健，具有更好抗震能力。
► 醫療與生技類股近一年漲幅明顯，主因1)生
技股過去一段時間受到高利率、融資成本及
臨床試驗風險壓抑，部分公司估值偏低。當
臨床數據改善、或導入AI篩選資料與藥物，
投資人重新評價未來現金流與價值。2)大型
藥廠面臨既有藥品專利到期壓力，透過收購
取得新藥與研發管線，提高有潛力的生技公
司併購價值，帶動整體產業估值回升。3)肥
胖、癌症與慢性病用藥創造長期市場需求，
創新藥物(如瘦瘦針)讓部分醫療公司成長不
再只依賴人口老化，高盛今年7月將全球減肥
藥市場2030年銷售額預估上調15%至1,140
億美元，看好此類藥品的長期商業潛力。
醫療生技近年表現佳，估今年3Q醫院客流量年增4%VIX恐慌指數上升時，健護醫療表現相較穩健

市場波動上升、醫療保健表現佳，獲利穩定成長

### Page 8

► 美國醫療費用深受法令影響，由於商業雇主
贊助保險(ESI)和平價醫療法案(ACA)的自付
額增加，以及最高自付費用要求，較去年同
期提高，使今年第三季美國整體住院人數、
手術量和醫院客流量均呈現改善。高盛預估
今年第三季醫院客流量年增4%，加上季節性
因素，下半年醫療保健利用率將較上半年成
長，整體第四季預估獲利穩健，甚至可延至
明年，反應在近期XLV ETF股價創新高。
► 展望2027年，各醫療保險計畫普遍優先考慮
透過提高會員保費，和要求費用分攤(尤其是
提高會員自付費用上限)來擴大利潤空間，並
持續優化覆蓋範圍和產品組合，預估商業雇
主提供的醫療保險(ESI)保費趨勢，將在明年
加速成長至過去20 年來的最高水準。 以
Refinitiv預估明年美國各產業獲利成長來看，
醫療保健和生技相較於今年獲利明顯增加，
反而今年獲利極佳的能源和通訊受高基期影
響，獲利增速下滑。投資人在整體金融環境
波動放大時期，宜以醫療生技股穩中求利。
投資焦點
近3個月漲幅和預估獲利呈正成長，醫療生技股佳

醫療保健和生技預估獲利明年明顯成長，有利股價續揚

股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長，市場靜待財報結果與展望，和對AI資本支出回收影響。
考量地緣衝突、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉，短期金融市場紛擾不止，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變，逢股市拉回時可分批布局，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點，全球利率走揚有利銀行股。此外，地緣衝突頻傳，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。

引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利
差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。

### Page 17

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書(或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用(境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金(不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施)；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

Charts of the week: Watching earnings and a potential ‘Blue wave’

Editorial 
Policy, politics and earnings  
Strategy summary: We retain our near-term defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, sectors and regions amid 
near-term uncertainty around monetary policy, politics and 
earnings. The next two weeks will be busy for major central 
banks, US political watchers and technology leaders powering 
the AI supercycle. We expect the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold 
rates next week, but markets will focus on whether central 
banks are turning more hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices. The US mid-term elections are expected to give 
Democrats control of the House and Senate, with implications 
for taxes and the AI-led investment boom.  
Meanwhile, the consensus is expecting the US tech sector to 
report over 60% y/y earnings growth for Q3, the biggest jump 
in over two decades outside the post-COVID rebound.  Each of 
these three drivers have the potential to surprise markets.  
Big Three to hold monetary policy: We expect the Fed, ECB 
and BoJ to hold policy rates next week, after September’s 
hikes, as they assess whether higher bond yields have 
tightened financial conditions enough to offset fuel-price-driven 
inflation. We expect the Big Three to hike next in December as 
fuel prices are likely to stay elevated into early next year due to 
refining capacity constraints. Nevertheless, the Fed and ECB 
are likely to deliver less than around 60bps of rate hikes priced 
in by money markets over the next 12 months on the back of 
an eventual easing of oil and fuel prices leading to disinflation 
by H2 2027. This outlook means 3-7-year Developed Market 
investment grade bonds currently offer attractive risk-reward.  
Opportunity in Australian bonds. Other major central banks, 
such as the Bank of England, Bank of Canada and Reserve 
Bank of New Zealand are likely to hike rates by 25bps in the 
next two weeks given their focus on taming near-term inflation. 
However, we believe the Reserve Bank of Australia (RBA) is 
close to the end of its hiking cycle, even if it hikes one last time 
in the coming months (money markets give a 60% chance of a 
hike on 3 November). Australia’s domestic labour and housing 
markets and business confidence are softening after the RBA 
hiked its policy rate four times this year to a 15-year high. 
Meanwhile, Australia’s cash rate is 60bps higher than the US 
policy rate, supporting the AUD. We continue to see attractive 
income and FX gain opportunities from AUD corporate bonds.  
A ‘Blue Wave’ in the US? Prediction markets strongly favour 
Democrats taking the House of Representatives and possibly 
the Senate in the 3 November US mid-term elections. While an 
opposition sweep in the mid-terms has historically delivered the 
best 12-month returns for equities among all scenarios, partly 
due to the resulting policy gridlock, a decisive Democratic “blue 
wave” could increase longer-term risks of higher taxes and 
uncertainty over policy towards AI investment and data centres. 
With the Senate race still close and prediction markets fallible, 
we remain cautious on risk assets in the near term.  
Closing EU bank contingent convertible bonds (CoCos1) 
and US 10-year government bond ideas: We lock in 2% 
gains in EU CoCos and 1.7% gain in US 10-year government 
bond ideas amid French and US political risks (page 5-6). We 
continue to hedge any inflation risks through inflation-protected 
bonds and geopolitical risk through an Overweight in gold.  
Watching Big Tech earnings:  Upcoming Big Tech earnings 
raises uncertainty as market leadership remains concentrated 
and share-price reactions more mixed. With valuations less 
likely to drive returns, earnings delivery and sustainability of AI 
investment, funding and monetisation and broadening out of 
earnings beyond tech will be critical, raising the risk of volatility 
and sector divergence. We remain invested but defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification across sectors and regions.  
Staying defensive and diversified. We recently upgraded the 
defensive US Healthcare sector to Overweight, while retaining 
our Overweights to US tech and materials as well as financials 
in the US, Euro area and Japan. We also downgraded the more 
volatile Communications Services sector to Neutral and took 
profits on the semiconductor segment earlier this month before 
the latest correction. Our defensive and diversified stance 
should preserve participation in further equity upside, while 
reducing exposure to earnings disappointments and supporting 
resilience through the reporting season.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the key takeaways from the technology sector 
earnings reported so far this week? 
Our view: Recent technology sector earnings results point to 
strong compute demand and continued capex expansion, 
supporting our positive long-term view on the AI investment 
theme. However, rising funding and execution risks warrant 
near-term caution. We prefer to avoid increasing exposure to 
higher-risk areas within the technology sector for now. 
Rationale: Earnings results reported this week suggest major tech 
companies remain committed to expanding their AI infrastructure. 
While this bodes well for AI infrastructure providers, ambitious 
expansion plans imply that an increasing share of AI capex is likely 
to depend on debt financing. We expect debt-financed AI capex to 
rise from c.25% of AI capex in 2026 to c.40% in 2027.  
Greater reliance on borrowing increases sensitivity to interest rates, 
while deployment delays could postpone revenue recognition amid 
ongoing financing costs. However, evidence of improving AI 
monetisation and adoption should strengthen operating cash 
flows and support internal capex funding. Against this backdrop, 
we will assess Big Tech earnings next week for greater clarity on 
capex guidance and funding and AI monetisation trends.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Euro area bank equities amid 
heightened political and fiscal uncertainty in the region? 
Our view: We remain positive on Euro area bank equities, 
supported by resilient earnings and shareholder-friendly capital 
return policies. While France’s fiscal uncertainty and a softer 
growth outlook warrant caution, the recent pullback in Euro 
area bank equities offers selective opportunities. We favour 
well-capitalised banks with strong deposit franchises and 
diversified earnings. 
Rationale: We expect Euro area banks’ Q3 2026 net interest 
income to grow 1.5-3.0% q/q, led by Spanish banks, driven by 
higher rates and continued asset repricing. Resilient fee income from 
wealth management and investment banking activity, as well as 
dividends and buybacks, should provide further support. Price-to-
book (P/B) valuations have eased, despite expectations for 
healthy return on equity (ROE), thus improving risk-reward. 
Fiscal uncertainty in France has widened sovereign spreads, 
pressuring banks’ bond holdings and funding costs. Although 
French banks represent 13% of the Euro area Bank Index, volatility 
could spread to regional financial markets. However, stress in the 
broader banking system appears limited, supported by strong 
capital buffers and ECB tools to contain disorderly market moves and 
sovereign market contagion. We will continue to monitor credit 
trends, sovereign spreads and banks’ 2027 earnings guidance. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What has driven the recent weakness in China equities, 
and what is your outlook from here? 
Our view: We maintain China equities as a Core holding, as 
valuations remain inexpensive. Rapid technological innovation 
and investment in AI localisation provide tailwinds, but the 
potential for a re-rating appears delayed. Investors should seek 
to diversify their portfolios and reduce concentration risk. 
Rationale: China equities have weakened, driven by mounting 
scepticism over AI capex returns, structural margin 
compression and concerns about the country’s significant 
financing requirements. Another notable feature in China has been 
significant share issuance, which has diluted earnings per share and 
weighed on the MSCI China Index’s performance. These pressures 
have been compounded by geopolitical headwinds, restrictions on 
advanced chips, an enduring property sector contraction and weak 
domestic demand. September’s stimulus measures also 
disappointed markets in terms of their scale and pace of delivery. 
Notwithstanding these headwinds, valuations remain inexpensive, 
supported by rapid domestic innovation and structural tailwinds 
in AI localisation, leading us to retain China equities as a Core 
holding. Elevated volatility reinforces the need to be defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification rather than relying on technology 
sector concentration. We also remain Overweight China’s 
healthcare, financial and materials sectors. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Are you still bullish on US 10-year government bonds?   
Our view: We are closing our opportunistic bullish idea on US 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. The risk-reward 
has become less compelling after the yield broke below 5%, the 
lower bound of our three-month forecast range of 5.00-5.25%. 
Rationale: The US 10-year yield fell over 17bps to 4.97% on 22 
October – its first move below 5% since 23 September and biggest 
one-day drop since March 2023. A sell-off in AI-related equities and 
renewed Middle East tensions drove investors towards safe havens. 
Earlier this month, the yield hit its highest level in over two decades. 
The forces behind the rally are fragile, though. Oil prices remain 
elevated amid escalating Middle East tensions, and September US 
consumer inflation rose to 3.7% y/y. Fed officials have signalled 
there is no urgency to raise rates at the 27-28 October meeting, 
but persistent price pressures keep further tightening on the table. 
We prefer to lock in gains and close our opportunistic bullish idea on 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO, Fixed Income & FX

Significant share issuance has kept the market 
capitalisation fairly stable, while the MSCI China

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for French bank bonds, especially 
ahead of France’s upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews?   
Our view: The recent sell-off in French bank bonds is a repricing 
of tail risks, not a credit story. We expect further price volatility 
until the April 2027 French presidential election, particularly at 
the junior end of the capital structure, so we are closing our 
opportunistic EU bank contingent convertibles (CoCos1) idea at 
a gain of 2.0%. 
Rationale: We believe the recent sell-off in French bank bonds 
reflects domestic political and fiscal risks rather than weaker 
credit fundamentals. The sustained widening of the OAT-Bund 
spread has raised concerns about the profit and loss (P&L) impact 
on banks' sovereign debt holdings. Banks provide limited 
disclosure on these exposures. However, banks’ investment 
portfolios are generally diversified, and holdings booked at amortised 
cost should soften the P&L impact. Near-term catalysts include the 
upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews. A sovereign downgrade 
could trigger bank rating downgrades, with domestically focused 
lenders potentially more exposed than regionally diversified peers. A 
sovereign credit event is not our base case, but we expect headline 
risks to persist until the French presidential election. Against this 
backdrop, we are closing our opportunistic EU bank CoCos idea. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Will the November US mid-term elections and European 
bond volatility support a sustained Swiss franc (CHF) rebound?  
Our view: We expect a near-term CHF rebound, supported 
mainly by safe-haven demand amid European bond volatility. 
We believe the US mid-term elections will be a secondary driver. 
Rationale: European sovereign bond volatility provides the stronger 
catalyst for CHF strength. The France-Germany government bond 
yield spread remains elevated near 160bps, reflecting heightened 
French fiscal concerns and reinforcing the CHF’s safe-haven appeal. 
The US mid-terms could provide additional support for the CHF. 
A Democratic victory in either chamber is expected to constrain 
further fiscal expansion, potentially weighing on the USD. However, 
this would not necessarily lead to faster fiscal consolidation. 
The SNB’s 0% policy rate leaves the CHF at a sizeable interest-
rate disadvantage vs. the USD, constraining sustained CHF gains. 
With EUR/CHF below 0.90, excessive CHF strength could also 
increase the risk of SNB intervention. A decisive USD/CHF break 
below 0.8130 would strengthen the technical case for a more 
sustained CHF rebound.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

A widening France-Germany 10-year government 
bond yield spread and a stable investment-grade 
(IG) bond option-adjusted spread (OAS) suggest a 
repricing of political and fiscal risk rather than 
credit risk

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would worsening budget deficits and political uncertainty 
across parts of Europe affect your view on EUR/USD and Euro 
area government bond yields? 
Our view: Fiscal and political concerns constrain near-term 
EUR/USD gains, with France being the primary vulnerability. We 
expect EUR/USD to recover towards the 1.17-1.18 range over 
the next 12 months, supported by continued Euro area 
economic growth and an expected fading of USD support. We 
expect wider differentials in Euro area government bond yields, 
with political risk premium elevated in French government 
bonds (OATs) and German government bonds (Bunds) 
supported by safe-haven demand. 
Rationale: France's high public debt, large budget deficit and 
political obstacles to fiscal adjustments are weighing on market 
confidence, with the country’s 10-year government bond yield 
rising above 5%. The risk of stress spreading to Italy and Spain 
adds to regional concerns, although current evidence does not 
establish a broad European fiscal crisis. German government bonds 
should benefit from safe-haven demand, while fiscal concerns keep 
risk premia on vulnerable countries' bonds elevated. EUR/USD 
remains more sensitive to US-Germany interest-rate differentials 
and broader market confidence than to French bond yields alone. 
France’s fiscal risks, elevated energy costs and high US interest 
rates weigh on the EUR in the near term. Over the next 12 months, 
Germany’s multi-year public investment programme, resilient 
exports and sound banking-sector fundamentals are expected to 
support Euro area growth. We expect the Fed's rate-hiking cycle to 
peak by mid-2027, reducing USD support and driving the EUR  
towards 1.17-1.18 . 
The principal tail risk is fiscal stress widening government bond 
yield spreads and spilling over into bank funding conditions and 
lending activity, weakening growth and delaying the EUR's recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

France’s fiscal risks constrain near-term EUR/USD 
gains, but a modest recovery remains likely

3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
It has been an action-packed few weeks for markets. The US
2026 mid-term elections saw a repudiation of President Trump
with the Democrats now set to take control over both houses
of Congress. The outcome and easing Middle East tensions

saw Brent crude oil fall from just under US$120/bbl to roughly
US$95/bbl, back around the post-Iran-conflict average. The
associated easing in inflation fears has supported a continued
descent in bond yields and rally in risk sentiment – albeit with
significant ‘wrinkles’ around a high US inflation read and an
AI-led pull-back in equity markets that has been compounded
by the sudden resignation of NVIDIA’s CEO.
Many central banks have already begun re-tightening policy

– with the Bank of Canada and Bank of England the latest
additions to the pack – but what comes next will differ widely
across jurisdictions. In the US, the combination of strong
activity and sticky inflation have led FOMC members to more
strongly advocate for further tightening. We have added a
January hike to the December move already in our forecasts,
before a long pause and eventual cuts in 2028.
Europe shares has similar inflation concerns but not so much

strength on the growth front. The ECB sees activity near
trend and is uncomfortable with inflation at 3.8%yr, so we
expect a December hike as insurance. Unlike market pricing
though, we think this will be the last, with energy and
investment-related price pressures easing through 2027 and
scope to ease back towards neutral in 2028. China faces a
very different challenge – not too much demand but too little.
Industrial strength and technology investment are providing

support but we expect policy direction and stimulus will be
needed to turn household demand, with this expected to
come in 2027 and property the most likely catalyst.
The RBA meanwhile followed its September hike with another
in November, but the Governor’s call to ‘take stock’ signals a
December pause and we think the hiking phase is now more
clearly over. We continue to pencil in a move to cuts in the
second half of 2027. While a February hike is not completely

out of the question, policy is clearly restrictive and downside
risks are crystallising. Reflecting this, we have downgraded
our GDP, labour market and housing market forecasts for 2027.
Australia: The housing downturn has been sharper than
expected and is set to spill over more materially to the broader
economy. The scale of these spillovers should become clearer
over coming months, keeping the RBA on hold as downside
risks to growth build. Business investment remains a source

of resilience, led by data centres and renewables, but rising
costs and growing community opposition have seen some
projects shelved. Still, the supply-side picture is constructive,
with strong investment in the market sector driving capital
deepening and productivity gains, albeit uneven ones.
Commodities: The commodities complex was a story of two
halves in October, with sharp gains across energy offsetting
weakness elsewhere and lifting our broadest commodities

index 0.6%mth. Reflecting continued energy-market tightness,
we expect Brent to average US$101/bbl in Q4 and have raised
our Q4 Japanese LNG forecast to US$27.
Global FX Markets: Of late, risks have favoured the US
dollar and FOMC member concerns have been decidedly one-
sided towards higher inflation. The DXY has consequently
continued to test the upper limit of its 18-month range, but
remains inside it.
New Zealand: A busy month has included a tight – and

currently inconclusive – general election, a surprise hold by the
RBNZ in October and a somewhat disappointing Q3 labour
market report. However, the medium-term outlook remains little
changed, with a recovering economy and above-target inflation
likely to lead to further monetary tightening in coming months.
Pacific: Distance from major markets, small domestic
economies and a heavy reliance on imported fuel and food
mean global disruptions tend to reach the Pacific at a higher

cost. However, most Pacific countries face the latest shock
with somewhat better buffers.
United States: Data suggests the US carried strong
momentum in to H2 2026. But questions are being asked over
the profitability of AI deployment, household real incomes are
trending flat to down, and term interest rates continue to weigh.
China: China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3, keeping
authorities on track for 2026, but more importantly offering a
little more time to accelerate domestic demand in to 2027.

Asia: We expect the BoJ to raise its policy rate by 25bps to
1.50% in December, before gradually taking it to 2.0% through
2027. The case for further tightening rests on the evidence that
firms are more willing to raise prices, labour scarcity supports
persistent wage growth, which should in turn support spending.
Europe & UK: The ECB left its key policy rates unchanged in
October. Another hike is forecast in December. Against market
pricing, we expect this will prove the last for the current cycle.

Taking stock

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
All major central banks are now in a rate-hiking phase. The
stance of policy nonetheless varies considerably across
economies, as does the policy outlook.
The Bank of England and Bank of Canada increased their
policy rates for the first time this cycle and the RBA a fifth time

since the last Market Outlook. The pace of increase is generally
moderate: the FOMC and RBNZ paused at their latest
meetings – the RBNZ unexpectedly so – but both are expected
to lift policy rates further. Policy is likely still accommodative
in New Zealand and Canada, consistent with the economic and
labour market slack evident in these economies. In Australia it
is clearly restrictive, as discussed below.
The US is contending with sticky inflation, as evidenced by

recent CPI and labour market data. We have long been of the
view that the US is likely to be structurally more inflationary
than many peers, and concerns about inflation are increasingly
coming to the fore in FOMC members’ comments. Accordingly
we have added another hike in January to our US rates view, on
top of the one in December already forecast (see page 20).
The main, but not sole, driver of the pivot to tightening has been
the energy price shock. That said, exclusion-based core inflation

measures (excluding food and energy) are also above central
bank targets. Market pricing of future central bank moves had
been sensitive to oil prices and the outlook in the Middle East
earlier in the year. That correlation broke down during the bond
sell-off, which was mostly a phenomenon of real yields, but
seems to have  re-emerged in the pricing for policy in 2027.
Spot and daated Brent oil prices have fallen markedly in recent
days as tensions have eased and negotiations appear to have

restarted. This has not affected very near-term pricing of central
bank decisions, and an initial moderation of mid-2027 pricing
retraced after the strong US CPI print and some Fed speeches.
Further along the yield curve, last month’s sell-off in bond
markets has largely reversed. Unlike the increase in yields,
when longer-run market-based measures of inflation
expectations relatively stable, the more recent rally has
involved some decline in these expectations measures. Most of

the adjustment has been front-loaded: the 1-year expectations
derived from swaps have declined 45bps for Australia and
nearly 60bps for the US since the beginning of November.
However, inflation expectations derived for future years have
also moderated. The 5-year-5-year forward measures derived
from swaps and breakevens have both declined, with the latter
noticeably below the 2.5% RBA target midpoint. The same
measures for the US have also declined over this period, but by

less, and remain further above the Fed’s 2% target, consistent
with our view about inflation pressures there.
Global asset market rotation ...
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
... while the RBA nears a pause
The rally in long bond markets has occurred at the same time as
equity markets have tended to sell off, particularly in the tech
sector. Another wave of scepticism around the sustainability
of the data centre build-out has formed and found expression

in the equity prices of many semiconductor manufacturers and
other related firms. One possible trigger for the latest sell-off
may have been the release of Anthropic’s prospectus ahead
of its IPO. As well as the more existential risks flagged in the
documents, its stated plans for further data centre investment
have concentrated focus on the scale of the data centre and AI
boom more broadly, and the enormous funding task involved.
The RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) hiked at its November

meeting following up the late September hike as expected. As
we noted at the time, the MPB was responding to the apparent
crystallisation of some of the risks that it had flagged back in
August. In particular, the re-emergence of pressures on fuel
prices led to fears of a further wave of pass-through of energy
costs into the prices of other goods and services. The Q3
outcome for underlying inflation outstripped expectations and
Q4 is also likely to be uncomfortably high. With inflation already

materially above target, the RBA is worried that inflation
expectations could be rising, pointing to liaison and business
survey evidence. So far, though, other business surveys suggest
that pass-through has been less severe than the original wave
of price increases at the outbreak of the Middle East conflict.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor flagged
that it was a good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening
to date. Both we and the market read this as a signal that the

MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote
at the meeting also suggests that some external members are
more attentive to downside risks than the internal members.
We think the most likely outcome from here is that the RBA
has ended its rate-hiking phase, and that a cutting phase will
begin in the second half of 2027. There is still a risk of a hike
in February, and we believe the MPB will at least discuss the
option at that meeting. The staff and senior officials remain

sensitive to inflation risks, and have a downbeat view of
supply capacity growth. The latter meant that the RBA had to
upgrade its forecasts for the labour force participation rate in
its However, policy is clearly restrictive, and while there are
upside risks to inflation, there are clearly downside risks, too.
With markets now expecting that the RBA is likely at the end
of its hiking phase, while most peers are expected to have 2–3
hikes to go, short-end yield differentials have narrowed. This

has been reflected in the AUD exchange rate, which has tended
to underperform in recent days. We have tempered our near-
term outlook for the AUD, but note that the TWI remains abourt
4% above the 2022–2024 average, and that this appreciation
will be contributing to the disinflationary impact of tight policy.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
The housing downturn has been sharper than expected and
is set to spill over more materially to the broader economy.
The scale of these spillovers should become clearer over
coming months, keeping the RBA on hold as downside risks

to growth build. Business investment remains a source of
resilience, led by data centres and renewables, but signs
of strain are emerging, with rising costs and growing
community opposition seeing some projects shelved. The
2026 Annual National Accounts offered a more constructive
supply-side picture, with strong investment across parts of
the market sector driving capital deepening and supporting
productivity, but this has been uneven.
As set out on page 26, we expect the lift in inflation this year

to subside through 2027, with underlying measures easing
back from 3.7%yr currently to 2.7%yr by end 2027 and 2.3%yr
in 2028. Indeed, the deeper housing downturn and associated
spillovers give us greater conviction in this view. The
correction is now the largest nominal price decline on records
going back to 1980 and appears to have accelerated following
the November 25bp RBA rate hike. This is on the back of
dismal dwelling finance approvals outcome which showed

the value of new loans plunged 15.5%qtr in Q3, the biggest
quarterly decline on records going back to 1991.
This downturn has some clear direct effects. The housing
sector, covering both real estate transactions and residential
construction, accounts for around 5–6% of GDP. The drop
in turnover will take about 0.4ppts off GDP growth over
calendar 2026 (captured through the “ownership transfer
costs” component of expenditure GDP) with the downturn also

expected to have a significant drag on dwelling construction
heading into 2027.
Steepest housing downturn in almost 50 years ...
Pat Bustamante, Senior Economist
However, the impact goes well beyond these direct effects.
Falling housing wealth will weigh on household spending,
while the residential building has strong linkages to the rest of
the economy. Residential investment supports activity across
manufacturing, transport and professional services; dollar

for dollar, we estimate its broader economic impact is around
1.5 times that of consumer spending. The flow on impacts via
employment and broader confidence are also substantive.
We have lowered our forecasts for household consumption,
ownership transfer costs and dwelling investment.
Consumption growth is now expected to trough at 1.1%yr
during 2027, from 1.8%yr forecast in our October Market
Outlook, before recovering to 2.7%yr in 2028. Ownership

transfer costs are expected to see a 25%yr decline over 2026,
the sharpest decline since the March quarter 1990, even
larger than what we saw during the pandemic. New dwelling
investment is expected to contract by 1.0%yr in 2027, rather
than grow by 2.1%yr as we expected back in October, before
recovering by 3.2%yr in 2028, down from 4.9%yr. Together
with some flow-on dampening effect on employment, these
revisions lower our GDP growth forecast to just 1.0%yr in

2027, from 1.6%yr, and 2.1%yr in 2028, from 2.5%yr.
We do not expect the slowdown to become immediately
apparent in the data. Consumer spending looks to have been
relatively resilient through the September quarter. Discretionary
services spend has been notably firm in particular, with only
some of this due to a catch-up on travel and tourism spend
disrupted by the Middle East conflict earlier in the year.
Household disposable incomes were still relatively well

supported through the quarter with some lift coming from tax
cuts and the FWC award wage increase. However, our card
tracker data suggests discretionary spending momentum has
faded noticeable over the first half of the December quarter.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
... to drive a sharp economic slowdown ...
The confidence impacts from housing downturn may also be
starting to show. While the drop in consumer sentiment back
to extreme lows has more to do with rate rises and fuel prices,
the weakening in business confidence became more prominent

in October with a notably sharp fall in forward orders. While
this has not had a flow-on impact on employment and capex
expectations to date, these will likely soften if confidence
remains negative and the signal from forward order starts to
be reflected in wider business conditions.
Business investment is still likely to be an important offset, with
data centres and renewables supporting activity. But even here,
some cracks are starting to emerge. Reports of project delays

in NSW and South Australia amid community opposition pose
limited risk to projects already underway but could increasingly
affect the pipeline of proposed data-centre investment (with
the notably cancellation of an IPO for a local operator as well).
Sentiment across the AI sector more broadly has also weakened
with equity valuations dropping, leadership instability at NVIDIA.
Anthropic’s ‘mega IPO’ due November 18 and expected to land
at market valuation of ~US$1trn will be an important test of how

fundamental the shift in sentiment may have been.
Annual National Accounts
The Annual National Accounts (the ‘Annuals’) provide updated
benchmarks and some useful detail including on investment
trends across industries, the capital stock and productivity.
The 2025–26 figures show somewhat firmer economic
activity and business investment than previously estimated,
particularly in 2024–25. The economy grew by 2.3% in
year-average terms, broadly in line with the initial quarterly

National Accounts estimate of 2.4%. However, growth in
2024–25 was revised up by 0.3ppts to 1.6%, largely reflecting
stronger private business investment. Business investment
is now estimated to have increased by 1.1% in 2024–25,
compared with the initial estimate of a 0.2% decline. The
revisions do not materially alter the broader productivity story,
but they do suggest that the economy was growing a little
more strongly but with the same degree of inflation in 2025.

The Annuals also provide a richer picture of the supply side,
including investment by industry and the evolution of the capital
stock, details that are not available in the quarterly accounts.
These data point to a notable lift in investment in the market (ex-
mining) part of the economy, where investment has gone from
around 10% of nominal GDP in to just over 11.5%. Outside of
this, investment has been softer, particularly in the mining sector.
Within the market sector, investment by the IT industry,

increasingly focused on data centres, has risen to its highest
share of nominal GDP since the technology boom of the
1990s. Utilities investment, including renewables and
associated infrastructure, is stronger still, at its highest share
since 1983, while investment by the professional services
industry has reached a record high. Across the non-market
sector (including health, education and public administration),
investment has also held at a slightly higher share of the

economy, driven by the health and public administration
(including defence) industries.
The investment upswing is also beginning to translate into
capital deepening as industries look to rebuild capital stocks
after a period of soft growth. Outside mining, market-sector
capital per hour worked is at a record high and well above its pre-
pandemic level. That is an encouraging development, particularly
given that the sector accounts for 70% of total employment.

There are important divergences beneath the aggregate. Mining
continues to experience capital shallowing, as investment has
been insufficient to cover wear and tear and capital requirements
associated with additional workers. By contrast, the capital stock
in the non-market sector appears to have caught up with the
surge in employment we have seen since the pandemic, with
capital per worker increasing for the first time since the pandemic.

### Page 8

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
Capital deepening will help support productivity growth
across the economy. The Annuals show that productivity in the
market sector (excluding mining) continues to grow by around
1% in year-average terms, while mining remains a drag on

aggregate productivity (down 0.8% in 2025–26 and almost
30% since 2017).
At the same time, a major recent headwind is easing as the rapid
expansion of the non-market sector slows. This matters for
aggregate productivity. Productivity growth reflects both gains
within industries, through capital deepening, technology and
better processes, and the ‘reallocation’ of resources between
industries with different productivity levels.
Measured productivity in the non-market sector is considerably

lower than across much of the market sector, partly reflecting
measurement issues (output is generally not well measured,
particularly when there are quality changes). As employment
shifted towards these industries, this compositional effect
weighed on aggregate productivity. Our estimates suggest it
has reduced the level of aggregate productivity by a significant
1ppt over the past 15 years and almost 0.3ppts over the three
years to 2025–26.
What drove growth in market-sector productivity?

It wasn’t only capital deepening that drove productivity growth.
Multifactor productivity (MFP), which captures how efficiently
businesses combine labour and capital through changes in
processes, technology, know-how, was the dominant driver of
market-sector productivity growth this year. This gives us more
conviction that the supply side is normalising, and productivity
growth will improve from its recent lows.
At the industry level, some of those recording stronger

investment are also beginning to deliver stronger productivity
outcomes, although the picture remains uneven.
... as non-mining sector rebuilds capital, productivity
IT stands out at the positive end of the spectrum, alongside
improving outcomes in manufacturing, administrative and
support services, and transport. These are also industries
where the diffusion of AI and other digital technologies could
deliver further productivity gains. Utilities and professional

services should also benefit as stronger investment translates
into greater capital intensity and efficiency.
At the other end, mining remains a major drag, consistent
with the capital shallowing evident in the investment and
capital-stock data. Construction is another notable laggard,
with productivity continuing to deteriorate despite strong
demand from housing, population growth, renewable-
energy infrastructure and data-centre projects. Construction

productivity fell around 2% in 2025–26 and almost 21% over
the 13 years from 2012–13 to 2025–26.
Bottom line
The Annuals provide a more constructive supply-side picture
than the aggregate productivity figures alone suggest. Market-
sector productivity outside mining continues to advance, the
reallocation drag from the expanding non-market sector looks
likely to fade, and capital deepening is strengthening across
important parts of the economy. The strong MFP outcome in

the market sector gives us more conviction that conviction that
the supply side is normalising, and productivity growth will
improve going ahead.
The critical question is whether the strong investment
underway in technology, professional services, electricity and
related infrastructure translates into stronger productivity
growth as that new capital is put to work. If it does, the
improvement in capital intensity evident in the Annuals
could mark the beginning of a more durable lift in Australia’s

productivity performance.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
The commodities complex was a story of two halves in
October, with energy prices rising sharply while most
other commodities weakened. Our broadest commodities
index gained 0.6%mth, led by Japanese LNG (+12.8%mth),
with Brent (+6.1%mth) and thermal coal (+2.9%mth) also

higher amid energy-market disruption. Copper edged
0.1%mth lower as AI sentiment weakened, while coking
coal (–3.5%mth) reversed recent gains and elevated yields
continued to weigh on gold (–5.0%mth). Iron ore extended
its slide (–6.1%mth), while aluminium fell 6.5%mth. Lithium
was the clear underperformer, with spodumene down
26.1%mth as environmental approval for CATL’s Jianxiawo
mine renewed oversupply concerns.
Oil supply returns, risk premium remains

Brent crude rose 6.1%mth in October to average US$106.2/bbl
as ongoing strikes and attacks on shipping through the Strait
of Hormuz continued to weigh on sentiment. Inventory buffers
remain depleted, with the EIA estimating OECD inventories
remain 141mb below pre-conflict levels in February. This comes
despite reported crude exports from Gulf producers recovering
to above 85% of year-ago levels in October. Reflecting improving
supply, Dated Brent moved to a discount to the front-month

futures contract at the start of November. Futures prices were
further subdued following the Republican defeat in the US
midterms and market expectations of a less intense US campaign
against Iran. Nonetheless, prices remain elevated, with fewer
tankers available after vessels were redeployed to other markets,
while higher insurance premiums continue to add to costs.
Refined product markets remain tight, with reduced Gulf supply
compounded by Ukrainian strikes on Russian refineries. Together

with higher shipping costs and elevated Dated Brent prices,
Singapore Gasoil 10ppm has roughly doubled since the Strait
closed, with the crack spread averaging around US$60.8/bbl in
the first week of November.
We do not expect a lasting US-Iran agreement until mid-2027.
In the meantime, we assume there will not be a return to full-
scale military intervention. However, periodic Iranian attacks on
ships and facilities are expected to continue, alongside the US

blockade of Iranian exports, maintaining a supply risk premium.
We expect Brent futures to average around US$101/bbl in Q4,
albeit with considerable volatility, before easing gradually as
recovering supply is partly offset by demand from refiners and
governments rebuilding inventories. As the risk premium fades
and the market moves into surplus in late 2027, we forecast
Brent at around US$77/bbl in Q4 2027. Refined product supply
is expected to recover more slowly than crude. For additional

analysis, including our October scenarios for further escalation
and an earlier resolution, see here.
Energy commodities push higher ...
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Luka Belobrajdic, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS

COMMODITIES
Qatar’s limited bypass options keep LNG tight
LNG prices continued to climb through October. Damage to
the world’s largest LNG export complex at Ras Laffan in Qatar
and the country’s inability to divert flows around the Strait of

Hormuz continue to weigh on supply. These constraints have
been compounded by stronger competition between Northeast
Asia and Europe for flexible Atlantic Basin cargoes ahead of
the Northern Hemisphere winter, with European gas storage
remaining below seasonal norms. As a result, Japanese LNG
prices rose 12.8%mth in October, averaging US$28.6/mmbtu.
Reflecting the continued acute disruption, we have raised our
December quarter forecast for Japanese LNG prices to US$27.5/

mmbtu. The severity of the Northern Hemisphere winter will
be the key risk to the near-term outlook. Supply recovery is
expected to remain constrained, with prices unlikely to return to
pre-conflict levels until 2028. Domestically, the Australian gas
market remains relatively insulated, supported by the FY2027
Federal Budget’s domestic gas reservation scheme, with an
objective to cap domestic gas prices at around $12/GJ.
Gold caught between yields and haven demand

Gold struggled through October, with prices down 5.0%mth to
average US$4,105/oz. As a non-yielding asset, gold has faced
broader pressure throughout the Middle East conflict as markets
weighed the inflationary implications of elevated energy prices
and the prospect of further central bank tightening. Despite
these headwinds, some pillars of support have emerged. A
meaningful bid has developed around US$4,000/oz, with
dip-buying supporting a recovery in gold ETF demand. Yields

also moved lower at the start of November prior to the US CPI
release as safe-haven demand increased against a backdrop of
macroeconomic, geopolitical and market uncertainty.
With these supports increasingly evident, we expect modest
gains through the remainder of 2026, with a December quarter
average of US$4,200/oz. From there, we expect a period
of consolidation through 2027 as brownfield expansions at
existing operations lift supply in response to recent high prices,

while an elevated Fed Funds rate limits upside for non-yielding
assets. Over the longer term, structural support should persist,
underpinned by growing Asian demand, and central bank buying.
Democrat sweep weighs on copper’s bull case
LME copper prices were broadly stable through October,
edging 0.1%mth lower to average US$14,478/t. The pace of
COMEX inventory accumulation slowed further to 2.9%mth
following reports in September that the US Government’s

decision on Section 232 tariff implementation had stalled
amid concerns higher copper prices could add to already
elevated manufacturing costs. Supply disruptions in Chile
provided more meaningful support, with two fatal accidents
at separate operations resulting in production suspensions in
a region already affected by weather disruptions. Alongside
broader electrification trends and metal shortages outside
the US, these disruptions supported copper through October.

However, a weaker macroeconomic outlook amid elevated
energy costs and a sharp rotation out of the copper-linked AI
trade left prices broadly unchanged over the month.
Several recent sources of support have come under pressure
in early November. The key shift has been the Democratic
sweep of Congress at the US midterm elections. The initial
equity-market reaction saw the AI trade pull back on concerns
that a Democrat-controlled Congress could impose greater

regulatory constraints on AI-infrastructure investment.
Copper’s exposure to the AI buildout made it a casualty of
the broader sell-off, with LME prices falling sharply at the
start of the month. A Democrat controlled Congress has also
reduced the perceived risk of more severe Section 232 tariff
implementation. Against this backdrop, we have downgraded
our near-term LME copper forecast, with prices expected to
average US$14,000/t in Q4. Ongoing electrification trends, AI

and data-centre investment, and metal shortages outside the
US should, however, continue to provide a floor under prices.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
The past month has seen a wave of risk events favouring the
US dollar. Meanwhile, concerns shown by FOMC members
have been decidedly one-sided, their clear message being
that the question is how many more hikes are needed. The
DXY has consequently continued to test the upper limit of

its 18-month range, circa 103, but has held to it.
Underlying the DXY’s movements are several key narratives.
Europe is the first port of call. Since mid-September, euro
has fallen from almost USD1.17 to a low near USD1.11.
Uncertainty surrounding France’s fiscal and economic outlook
was part of that turn, however, most of the move aligns to
concerns amongst market participants over the persistence of
inflation and the resetting of policy rate expectations. Albeit

marginal, expected fed fund rate hikes outnumber those for
the ECB, entrenching short and long-end rate differentials.
Sterling has held up better than euro, falling from around
USD1.36 in September to near USD1.32. But, to our mind, the
chances of the sequence of rate hikes priced by the market for
the Bank of England is low. After November’s policy meeting,
Governor Bailey showed caution over the activity outlook
and a greater willingness to incorporate the recent drop in oil

prices into inflation forecasts than FOMC members have.
Interest rates are not the only comparable favouring the US
dollar; each nation’s growth story is also important. While
Europe and the UK have recently delivered economic outcomes
consistent with a return to trend growth, US data continues
to point to momentum in excess of potential. Moreover, as
discussed on page 19, the pipeline of AI related investment
and funding continues to strengthen, favouring the US.

It is intriguing then that the US dollar has not broken the range
of the past 18 months. Based on this performance, lingering
downside risks for the US economic outlook and the potential
for a more constructive outturn for energy, we remain of the
view that the US dollar is likely to recommence its downtrend
in mid-2027, once this monetary policy cycle definitively ends.
For euro, a slow (but likely volatile) uptrend is anticipated,
from USD1.11 today to USD1.17 at end-2027 then USD1.21

come end-2028. Sterling is similarly forecast to lift from
USD1.32 at present to USD1.38 at end-2027 and then
USD1.41 by late-2028 – seeing broad stability between
euro and sterling. Dissipating global risks over the coming
two years should also result in both euro and sterling lifting
against the Swiss franc – albeit only at the margin.
The primary risk to this view is that appreciation against the
US dollar comes later. Though, as we are only targeting

US dollar support ...
Elliot Clarke
